They’ve been quietly interfering with our money and with my reputation, our good name, you know?\n\nIt’s not one big obvious thing where I can just point to it and say, “There, that’s it.” It’s little things that keep happening. Like at work, I’m a billing clerk, so I know when numbers don’t look right, and these little billing errors keep showing up in ways that make me look careless. And then someone will pull up records and say, “Oh, see, it’s nothing,” or “That was just a mistake,” but I know it ties back to them somehow.\n\nAnd then at church, people who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly act strange, like short with me or like they’ve heard something. Nobody comes right out and says it, of course, but that’s kind of the point. It’s all done quietly. I just need someone to understand that this isn’t me being dramatic. It’s been going on for months, and I’m tired of being treated like I’m the problem when I’m the one noticing what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's family is intentionally trying to harm you or your reputation?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I don’t think it’s accidental anymore, not after how long it’s been going on and how the same kinds of things keep happening in different places. It’s not like they came up to me and said, “We’re going to ruin your name,” obviously they wouldn’t do that. It’s quieter than that.\n\nIt’s things with our money, and then things that make me look bad. Like at work, with the billing errors—small ones, just enough that if someone looks quickly, I seem careless or incompetent. And then people act like, “Well, the records don’t prove anything,” but I work with billing. I know when things don’t sit right. And then at church, people who used to be warm with me suddenly get distant or look at me a certain way, like they’ve heard something about me.\n\nSo yes, I believe his family is behind it. Maybe not in some loud obvious way, but intentionally, yes. It feels like they’re trying to damage how people see me and interfere with what we have.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're the only one being targeted by your husband's family, or could others be affected as well?\n\nPatient: I think I’m the one they’re focusing on, mostly, because it’s my name that keeps getting touched, if that makes sense. It’s our money too, yes, but the way it shows up is always like it’s meant to make me look irresponsible or careless. Like those little billing things at work—small enough that someone else can say, “Oh, that’s just an error,” but placed in a way where I’m the one who looks bad.\n\nAnd at church, it’s me people act funny toward. They don’t say anything outright, which is part of why it’s so hard to explain, but I can feel the shift. People who used to be friendly suddenly get stiff or short, like they’ve heard something about me or been told to keep their distance.\n\nI suppose other people could be affected, because if they’re messing with our finances, that touches my husband too. But the reputation part—the good name part—it feels aimed at me. I’m the one everyone keeps trying to convince is “overreacting,” even though these things have been happening for months and they connect back to his family.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that your husband's family is behind these actions?\n\nPatient: Proof in the way everyone wants, like a signed note or someone admitting it? No, I don’t have that. That’s part of what makes this so frustrating, because every time I try to explain it, people act like if there isn’t one single piece of paper that says, “Yes, we did this,” then it must not be happening.\n\nBut I do have what I’m seeing over and over. For months now, these little things keep happening with our money and with my name. At work, the billing errors don’t feel random to me. I’m a billing clerk, I know how records are supposed to look, and these mistakes show up in ways that make me look careless. Then when someone pulls up the records, they say it’s just a small error or nothing can be proven, but that doesn’t mean there’s no pattern.\n\nAnd at church, people who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly act strange—short, distant, like they’ve heard something. Nobody says it outright, of course. That’s the whole point. It’s quiet. It’s the kind of thing where if you describe one piece by itself, people can dismiss it. But when it keeps happening in different places, and it all seems to come back around to my husband’s family and our good name, I know what it is.\n\nSo no, I don’t have the kind of proof other people keep demanding. But I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these actions by your husband's family could be happening without you noticing or realizing it?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s exactly how they do it—quietly, where I don’t always see it happening right in front of me. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. It’s not like they come to my house and announce it, or do something obvious where I can point and say, “There, that’s them.” It’s more like things happen around me, and then I’m left dealing with the result.\n\nLike with the money, or the little errors that show up at work. I don’t necessarily see someone changing something in the moment, but then there it is, and somehow it reflects badly on me. And at church, same thing—someone suddenly acts funny toward me, or distant, and I don’t know exactly what was said to them, but I can tell something has shifted. People want me to ignore that, but when it keeps happening for months, in different places, it isn’t just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I think some of it could be happening without me realizing it right away. That’s part of what makes it so upsetting. I notice after—the look someone gives me, the way a record doesn’t sit right, the way my good name gets chipped away at little by little. And no matter what records people pull up, I know it ties back to my husband’s family.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your husband's family might not be behind these things you've been noticing?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose if you’re asking me in a general way, could there be some other explanation for one little thing here or there, then maybe. I’m not saying every single odd look from every person in the world is them personally doing something. But when you put it all together, after months of it, I don’t really see how I’m supposed to believe it’s just random.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, honestly. I’ve been trying to get people to understand that it’s not one isolated billing error or one person at church being a little cool toward me. It’s the way these things keep happening and they all touch the same areas—our money, my reputation, our good name. At work, small billing things come up in a way that makes me look careless, and then if records get pulled, people say, “Well, this doesn’t prove anything.” And at church, someone who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly acts stiff or funny, like they’ve heard something they’re not saying out loud.\n\nSo could someone tell me, “Maybe it isn’t your husband’s family”? Yes, people tell me that all the time. But no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. It’s too connected, and it’s been going on too long for me to just shrug it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could convince you that your husband's family is not behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me, honestly, because every time someone says they’ve checked something, it’s like they’re only checking the obvious part. They pull up a record or look at one billing issue and say, “See, there’s no proof,” but that doesn’t explain why these things keep happening around my money and my name.\n\nI’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is doing this quietly. Not in a way where they leave fingerprints all over it, but in the way little errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly starts acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then I’m the one who looks careless, or unstable, or like I’m imagining it.\n\nSo if you’re asking, could there be some record that would make me say, “Oh, it definitely wasn’t them”? I don’t really see how, because the records are exactly the kind of thing that can miss what’s really going on. No matter what anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. It’s the pattern, and it’s been going on too long.\n\nDoctor: Could anything change your mind about your husband's family being behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think about that, I really have, because people keep asking me like there must be some simple answer that would make me drop it. But the problem is, when someone says, “Well, we checked the records,” or “That billing issue was just a mistake,” it doesn’t cover the whole thing. It’s not just one record or one mistake.\n\nI’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is doing this quietly, in ways that don’t look obvious if you only look at each thing by itself. Like the little billing errors that show up at work and somehow make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they know something or heard something about me. And then if I say anything, I’m the one who sounds like I’m overreacting.\n\nSo could something change my mind? I don’t know. Maybe if all of it stopped completely and people went back to treating me normal, and there weren’t these little things with money or my good name being picked at. But as far as someone just pulling up records and saying, “See, it isn’t them,” no. That wouldn’t change it for me, because I know it ties back to them. It’s the pattern of it. That’s what people keep missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done anything to unintentionally contribute to your husband's family's behavior towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, because I don’t want to pretend I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. I suppose maybe I’ve been too direct at times, or maybe I noticed things and started asking questions, and they didn’t like that. But I don’t think anything I did justifies what’s been going on.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not like they come out and say it plainly. It’s little things, like billing errors showing up at work that make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something they’re not saying. And then when I try to explain it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo if you mean, did I unintentionally cause it somehow… maybe by not just keeping quiet, maybe by standing up for myself, I don’t know. But no, I don’t think I caused them to do this. They chose to do it. And no matter what records anyone pulls up or how they try to explain away each little piece, I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting some of these events or signs?\n\nPatient: I understand why you’re asking that, and I’ve tried to be fair about it. I know people can misread things sometimes. If it was just one thing, like one billing error, maybe I could say, alright, maybe I’m taking it the wrong way. Or if one person at church seemed off one Sunday, maybe they were just having a bad day.\n\nBut that’s not what this feels like. It’s been months of these little things, and they all circle back to the same place. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not loud or obvious, it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re doing this.” It’s the way little billing problems show up at work and make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say it.\n\nSo, could I be misinterpreting one small thing here or there? Maybe, in theory. I’m not saying I never misunderstand anything. But the whole pattern? No, I don’t think I’m wrong about that. And when people just pull up records and say, “See, there’s nothing there,” that doesn’t really answer it for me, because those records don’t show the quiet parts. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to consider an alternative explanation for the events you've described, other than your husband's family being behind them?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can try to consider it, because I know that’s what you’re asking me to do, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I’ve gone over it in my head so many times, actually. Like, could the billing errors at work just be ordinary mistakes? Could someone at church just be in a bad mood or distracted? Could I be connecting things that other people don’t see connected? I understand those are possibilities if you look at each piece by itself.\n\nBut that’s exactly the problem. Everyone keeps looking at each thing separately, like one little accounting problem here, one strange look there, one conversation that changes when I walk up. And I’m telling you, when you’re living inside it, it doesn’t feel separate. It feels coordinated. It feels like it keeps coming back to my husband’s family, even if they’re careful enough that it doesn’t show plainly on paper.\n\nSo yes, I can say the words that there could be another explanation for one or two things. But for the whole thing? No, not really. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. And no matter what records anyone pulls up, that doesn’t erase the pattern. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have any enemies or people who might want to harm you outside of your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Outside of his family? No, not really. I mean, I can’t say I have enemies in the way people talk about enemies, like someone openly threatening me or saying they want to hurt me. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is his family. That’s where it ties together. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not dramatic, it’s not somebody standing on my porch yelling at me. It’s little things that make me look bad or make life harder, like billing errors showing up at work in a way that makes me seem careless, or people at church suddenly acting different toward me, like they’ve heard something but won’t say what.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s some other person out there I think is after me, I don’t think so. It’s more that other people may get pulled into it, or they may believe things they’ve been told, but the source of it, the people behind it, I really believe that’s my husband’s family. And even when people pull up records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. It still comes back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done something to provoke your husband's family into behaving this way towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, honestly. I’m not sitting here saying I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. Families have disagreements, and with in-laws there are always little tensions, you know? Maybe I didn’t handle every conversation exactly right, maybe I spoke up when they thought I should stay quiet, maybe they didn’t like how my husband and I did certain things.\n\nBut provoke them? Like I did something that would justify all this? No. I don’t think so. Nothing that would explain months of this quiet pressure, where it feels like they’re messing with our money and our good name. That’s the part I keep trying to get people to understand. It’s not one big fight where I can point to it and say, “That’s when it started.” It’s these little things that keep happening — billing problems showing up at work that make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting strange toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say it.\n\nSo maybe they have their reasons in their own minds. Maybe they think I took my husband away from them, or they don’t like that I notice things. I don’t know exactly what excuse they use. But I don’t believe I did anything to deserve this. And even when people pull records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t mean nothing is happening. The pattern still points back to them.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or stressful happening in your life before you started noticing these issues with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, there was stress before I really started noticing the pattern. It wasn’t like everything was peaceful and then out of nowhere I decided something was wrong. It started around the trouble with my husband’s family over money and the holidays — expectations about who should pay for what, where we were supposed to be, what we were supposed to do. Those kinds of things.\n\nAnd then after that, over the months, I started seeing how things were connecting. I’d say it’s been going on about nine months now. That’s when it really began to feel like more than ordinary family tension. At first I tried to tell myself, “Okay, families argue, people get upset around holidays, money makes people act funny.” But it didn’t settle down. It kept continuing in these little ways, and that’s when I started feeling like they were involved in what was happening.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation or evidence that would make you question your belief that your husband's family is behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would make me question it, honestly, because people keep acting like if they pull up one record and it looks normal, then that should settle the whole thing. But that doesn’t really answer what I’m talking about.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not one obvious thing where someone signs their name to it. It’s little things, like a billing error showing up at work in a way that makes me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then when I try to explain it, people say, “Well, the records don’t show anything,” or “Maybe it’s just a mistake.” But I know how these things feel when they keep happening and they all point in the same direction.\n\nSo, evidence that would make me question it… I guess if there was some clear explanation for all of it that didn’t involve them, maybe. But so far nobody has given me one that makes sense. They just tell me there’s nothing there, and that feels like missing the point. Because no matter what records anyone pulls up, I still know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts like this before, where you felt like someone was out to get you or sabotaging you?\n\nPatient: Not like this, no. I mean, I’ve had times in life where I felt someone didn’t like me, or where there was tension with people, sure. Everybody has that. But this is different.\n\nThis has been months now of me trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly interfering with our money and our good name. It’s not like I woke up one day and said, “Everyone is against me.” It’s more like one thing happened, then another thing, then another, and after a while you can’t keep calling everything a coincidence. Like little billing errors showing up at work in ways that make me look careless, or people at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something they’re not telling me.\n\nBefore all this, I don’t remember having thoughts like that, not in this same way. I wasn’t walking around thinking people were out to get me. But now, with this situation, no matter what records anyone pulls up or how many people say they don’t see it, I know it ties back to them. That’s the part that makes it so upsetting, because it feels like I’m the only one putting the pieces together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your belief about your husband's family being behind these actions, no matter what evidence or explanations you're given?\n\nPatient: Honestly… I don’t think so. I know that probably sounds stubborn, but it’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It’s just that I’ve lived with this for months now, and I’ve watched the same kinds of things keep happening in different places.\n\nPeople keep saying, “Well, the records look fine,” or, “That could just be a billing mistake,” or, “Maybe that person at church was just having a bad day.” But when little billing errors show up at work in a way that makes me look careless, and then someone at church suddenly acts strange toward me like they’ve heard something, and then another thing happens with money or with my name… after a while it all connects.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether some explanation could change my mind, I guess I’m saying I don’t see how. Because no matter what records someone pulls up, I still know it ties back to my husband’s family. They’re careful about it. That’s the whole point. It’s not going to be sitting there in plain writing with their names on it. But I know they’re behind it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is his family. That’s where it ties together. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not dramatic, it’s not somebody standing on my porch yelling at me. It’s little things that make me look bad or make life harder, like billing errors showing up at work in a way that makes me seem careless, or people at church suddenly acting different toward me, like they’ve heard something but won’t say what.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s some other person out there I think is after me, I don’t think so. It’s more that other people may get pulled into it, or they may believe things they’ve been told, but the source of it, the people behind it, I really believe that’s my husband’s family. And even when people pull up records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. It still comes back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done something to provoke your husband's family into behaving this way towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, honestly. I’m not sitting here saying I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. Families have disagreements, and with in-laws there are always little tensions, you know? Maybe I didn’t handle every conversation exactly right, maybe I spoke up when they thought I should stay quiet, maybe they didn’t like how my husband and I did certain things.\n\nBut provoke them? Like I did something that would justify all this? No. I don’t think so. Nothing that would explain months of this quiet pressure, where it feels like they’re messing with our money and our good name. That’s the part I keep trying to get people to understand. It’s not one big fight where I can point to it and say, “That’s when it started.” It’s these little things that keep happening — billing problems showing up at work that make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting strange toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say it.\n\nSo maybe they have their reasons in their own minds. Maybe they think I took my husband away from them, or they don’t like that I notice things. I don’t know exactly what excuse they use. But I don’t believe I did anything to deserve this. And even when people pull records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t mean nothing is happening. The pattern still points back to them.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or stressful happening in your life before you started noticing these issues with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, there was stress before I really started noticing the pattern. It wasn’t like everything was peaceful and then out of nowhere I decided something was wrong. It started around the trouble with my husband’s family over money and the holidays — expectations about who should pay for what, where we were supposed to be, what we were supposed to do. Those kinds of things.\n\nAnd then after that, over the months, I started seeing how things were connecting. I’d say it’s been going on about nine months now. That’s when it really began to feel like more than ordinary family tension. At first I tried to tell myself, “Okay, families argue, people get upset around holidays, money makes people act funny.” But it didn’t settle down. It kept continuing in these little ways, and that’s when I started feeling like they were involved in what was happening.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation or evidence that would make you question your belief that your husband's family is behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would make me question it, honestly, because people keep acting like if they pull up one record and it looks normal, then that should settle the whole thing. But that doesn’t really answer what I’m talking about.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not one obvious thing where someone signs their name to it. It’s little things, like a billing error showing up at work in a way that makes me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then when I try to explain it, people say, “Well, the records don’t show anything,” or “Maybe it’s just a mistake.” But I know how these things feel when they keep happening and they all point in the same direction.\n\nSo, evidence that would make me question it… I guess if there was some clear explanation for all of it that didn’t involve them, maybe. But so far nobody has given me one that makes sense. They just tell me there’s nothing there, and that feels like missing the point. Because no matter what records anyone pulls up, I still know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts like this before, where you felt like someone was out to get you or sabotaging you?\n\nPatient: Not like this, no. I mean, I’ve had times in life where I felt someone didn’t like me, or where there was tension with people, sure. Everybody has that. But this is different.\n\nThis has been months now of me trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly interfering with our money and our good name. Like little billing errors showing up at work in ways that make me look careless, or people at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something they’re not telling me.\n\nBefore all this, I don’t remember having thoughts like that, not in this same way. I wasn’t walking around thinking people were out to get me. But now, with this situation, no matter what records anyone pulls up or how many people say they don’t see it, I know it ties back to them. That’s the part that makes it so upsetting, because it feels like I’m the only one putting the pieces together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your belief about your husband's family being behind these actions, no matter what evidence or explanations you're given?\n\nPatient: Honestly… I don’t think so. I know that probably sounds stubborn, but it’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It’s just that I’ve lived with this for months now, and I’ve watched the same kinds of things keep happening in different places.\n\nPeople keep saying, “Well, the records look fine,” or, “That could just be a billing mistake,” or, “Maybe that person at church was just having a bad day.” But when little billing errors show up at work in a way that makes me look careless, and then someone at church suddenly acts strange toward me like they’ve heard something, and then another thing happens with money or with my name… after a while it all connects.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether some explanation could change my mind, I guess I’m saying I don’t see how. Because no matter what records someone pulls up, I still know it ties back to my husband’s family. They’re careful about it. That’s the whole point. I mean… I’ve thought about what people are trying to say when they ask me that, yes. Like, “Could this be in your head?” But that doesn’t feel right to me, because these are actual things that have happened. It’s not like I’m just sitting at home imagining problems out of nowhere.

And it’s always done in these small ways that are easy for other people to brush off. Like a billing error shows up at work and suddenly I look careless, or someone at church starts acting strange toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say what. And then if I bring it up, people want to say, “Well, maybe that was just a mistake,” or, “Maybe you misunderstood.” But when it keeps happening, over and over, it’s hard to believe it’s just my own mind making connections.

So no, I don’t really think it’s just coming from me. I know people may not see it the way I do, and I know the records may not show their names written on anything, but that doesn’t mean they aren’t behind it. They’re careful. That’s why it’s so hard to prove. But I know it ties back to them.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like I’ve been trying for months to get somebody to really listen to me, and everyone keeps acting like I’m making too much of it. It has to do with my husband’s family. They’ve been quietly interfering with our money and with my reputation, our good name, you know?\n\nIt’s not one big obvious thing where I can just point to it and say, “There, that’s it.” It’s little things that keep happening. Like at work, I’m a billing clerk, so I know when numbers don’t look right, and these little billing errors keep showing up in ways that make me look careless. And then someone will pull up records and say, “Oh, see, it’s nothing,” or “That was just a mistake,” but I know it ties back to them somehow.\n\nAnd then at church, people who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly act strange, like short with me or like they’ve heard something. Nobody comes right out and says it, of course, but that’s kind of the point. It’s all done quietly. I just need someone to understand that this isn’t me being dramatic. It’s been going on for months, and I’m tired of being treated like I’m the problem when I’m the one noticing what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's family is intentionally trying to harm you or your reputation?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I don’t think it’s accidental anymore, not after how long it’s been going on and how the same kinds of things keep happening in different places. It’s not like they came up to me and said, “We’re going to ruin your name,” obviously they wouldn’t do that. It’s quieter than that.\n\nIt’s things with our money, and then things that make me look bad. Like at work, with the billing errors—small ones, just enough that if someone looks quickly, I seem careless or incompetent. And then people act like, “Well, the records don’t prove anything,” but I work with billing. I know when things don’t sit right. And then at church, people who used to be warm with me suddenly get distant or look at me a certain way, like they’ve heard something about me.\n\nSo yes, I believe his family is behind it. Maybe not in some loud obvious way, but intentionally, yes. It feels like they’re trying to damage how people see me and interfere with what we have.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're the only one being targeted by your husband's family, or could others be affected as well?\n\nPatient: I think I’m the one they’re focusing on, mostly, because it’s my name that keeps getting touched, if that makes sense. It’s our money too, yes, but the way it shows up is always like it’s meant to make me look irresponsible or careless. Like those little billing things at work—small enough that someone else can say, “Oh, that’s just an error,” but placed in a way where I’m the one who looks bad.\n\nAnd at church, it’s me people act funny toward. They don’t say anything outright, which is part of why it’s so hard to explain, but I can feel the shift. People who used to be friendly suddenly get stiff or short, like they’ve heard something about me or been told to keep their distance.\n\nI suppose other people could be affected, because if they’re messing with our finances, that touches my husband too. But the reputation part—the good name part—it feels aimed at me. I’m the one everyone keeps trying to convince is “overreacting,” even though these things have been happening for months and they connect back to his family.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that your husband's family is behind these actions?\n\nPatient: Proof in the way everyone wants, like a signed note or someone admitting it? No, I don’t have that. That’s part of what makes this so frustrating, because every time I try to explain it, people act like if there isn’t one single piece of paper that says, “Yes, we did this,” then it must not be happening.\n\nBut I do have what I’m seeing over and over. For months now, these little things keep happening with our money and with my name. At work, the billing errors don’t feel random to me. I’m a billing clerk, I know how records are supposed to look, and these mistakes show up in ways that make me look careless. Then when someone pulls up the records, they say it’s just a small error or nothing can be proven, but that doesn’t mean there’s no pattern.\n\nAnd at church, people who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly act strange—short, distant, like they’ve heard something. Nobody says it outright, of course. That’s the whole point. It’s quiet. It’s the kind of thing where if you describe one piece by itself, people can dismiss it. But when it keeps happening in different places, and it all seems to come back around to my husband’s family and our good name, I know what it is.\n\nSo no, I don’t have the kind of proof other people keep demanding. But I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these actions by your husband's family could be happening without you noticing or realizing it?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s exactly how they do it—quietly, where I don’t always see it happening right in front of me. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. It’s not like they come to my house and announce it, or do something obvious where I can point and say, “There, that’s them.” It’s more like things happen around me, and then I’m left dealing with the result.\n\nLike with the money, or the little errors that show up at work. I don’t necessarily see someone changing something in the moment, but then there it is, and somehow it reflects badly on me. And at church, same thing—someone suddenly acts funny toward me, or distant, and I don’t know exactly what was said to them, but I can tell something has shifted. People want me to ignore that, but when it keeps happening for months, in different places, it isn’t just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I think some of it could be happening without me realizing it right away. That’s part of what makes it so upsetting. I notice after—the look someone gives me, the way a record doesn’t sit right, the way my good name gets chipped away at little by little. And no matter what records people pull up, I know it ties back to my husband’s family.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your husband's family might not be behind these things you've been noticing?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose if you’re asking me in a general way, could there be some other explanation for one little thing here or there, then maybe. I’m not saying every single odd look from every person in the world is them personally doing something. But when you put it all together, after months of it, I don’t really see how I’m supposed to believe it’s just random.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, honestly. I’ve been trying to get people to understand that it’s not one isolated billing error or one person at church being a little cool toward me. It’s the way these things keep happening and they all touch the same areas—our money, my reputation, our good name. At work, small billing things come up in a way that makes me look careless, and then if records get pulled, people say, “Well, this doesn’t prove anything.” And at church, someone who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly acts stiff or funny, like they’ve heard something they’re not saying out loud.\n\nSo could someone tell me, “Maybe it isn’t your husband’s family”? Yes, people tell me that all the time. But no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. It’s too connected, and it’s been going on too long for me to just shrug it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could convince you that your husband's family is not behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me, honestly, because every time someone says they’ve checked something, it’s like they’re only checking the obvious part. They pull up a record or look at one billing issue and say, “See, there’s no proof,” but that doesn’t explain why these things keep happening around my money and my name.\n\nI’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is doing this quietly. Not in a way where they leave fingerprints all over it, but in the way little errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly starts acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then I’m the one who looks careless, or unstable, or like I’m imagining it.\n\nSo if you’re asking, could there be some record that would make me say, “Oh, it definitely wasn’t them”? I don’t really see how, because the records are exactly the kind of thing that can miss what’s really going on. No matter what anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. It’s the pattern, and it’s been going on too long.\n\nDoctor: Could anything change your mind about your husband's family being behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think about that, I really have, because people keep asking me like there must be some simple answer that would make me drop it. But the problem is, when someone says, “Well, we checked the records,” or “That billing issue was just a mistake,” it doesn’t cover the whole thing. It’s not just one record or one mistake.\n\nI’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is doing this quietly, in ways that don’t look obvious if you only look at each thing by itself. Like the little billing errors that show up at work and somehow make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they know something or heard something about me. And then if I say anything, I’m the one who sounds like I’m overreacting.\n\nSo could something change my mind? I don’t know. Maybe if all of it stopped completely and people went back to treating me normal, and there weren’t these little things with money or my good name being picked at. But as far as someone just pulling up records and saying, “See, it isn’t them,” no. That wouldn’t change it for me, because I know it ties back to them. It’s the pattern of it. That’s what people keep missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done anything to unintentionally contribute to your husband's family's behavior towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, because I don’t want to pretend I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. I suppose maybe I’ve been too direct at times, or maybe I noticed things and started asking questions, and they didn’t like that. But I don’t think anything I did justifies what’s been going on.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not like they come out and say it plainly. It’s little things, like billing errors showing up at work that make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something they’re not saying. And then when I try to explain it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo if you mean, did I unintentionally cause it somehow… maybe by not just keeping quiet, maybe by standing up for myself, I don’t know. But no, I don’t think I caused them to do this. They chose to do it. And no matter what records anyone pulls up or how they try to explain away each little piece, I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting some of these events or signs?\n\nPatient: I understand why you’re asking that, and I’ve tried to be fair about it. I know people can misread things sometimes. If it was just one thing, like one billing error, maybe I could say, alright, maybe I’m taking it the wrong way. Or if one person at church seemed off one Sunday, maybe they were just having a bad day.\n\nBut that’s not what this feels like. It’s been months of these little things, and they all circle back to the same place. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not loud or obvious, it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re doing this.” It’s the way little billing problems show up at work and make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say it.\n\nSo, could I be misinterpreting one small thing here or there? Maybe, in theory. I’m not saying I never misunderstand anything. But the whole pattern? No, I don’t think I’m wrong about that. And when people just pull up records and say, “See, there’s nothing there,” that doesn’t really answer it for me, because those records don’t show the quiet parts. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to consider an alternative explanation for the events you've described, other than your husband's family being behind them?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can try to consider it, because I know that’s what you’re asking me to do, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I’ve gone over it in my head so many times, actually. Like, could the billing errors at work just be ordinary mistakes? Could someone at church just be in a bad mood or distracted? Could I be connecting things that other people don’t see connected? I understand those are possibilities if you look at each piece by itself.\n\nBut that’s exactly the problem. Everyone keeps looking at each thing separately, like one little accounting problem here, one strange look there, one conversation that changes when I walk up. And I’m telling you, when you’re living inside it, it doesn’t feel separate. It feels coordinated. It feels like it keeps coming back to my husband’s family, even if they’re careful enough that it doesn’t show plainly on paper.\n\nSo yes, I can say the words that there could be another explanation for one or two things. But for the whole thing? No, not really. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. And no matter what records anyone pulls up, that doesn’t erase the pattern. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have any enemies or people who might want to harm you outside of your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Outside of his family? No, not really. I mean, I can’t say I have enemies in the way people talk about enemies, like someone openly threatening me or saying they want to hurt me. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is his family. That’s where it ties together. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not dramatic, it’s not somebody standing on my porch yelling at me. It’s little things that make me look bad or make life harder, like billing errors showing up at work in a way that makes me seem careless, or people at church suddenly acting different toward me, like they’ve heard something but won’t say what.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s some other person out there I think is after me, I don’t think so. It’s more that other people may get pulled into it, or they may believe things they’ve been told, but the source of it, the people behind it, I really believe that’s my husband’s family. And even when people pull up records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. It still comes back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done something to provoke your husband's family into behaving this way towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, honestly. I’m not sitting here saying I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. Families have disagreements, and with in-laws there are always little tensions, you know? Maybe I didn’t handle every conversation exactly right, maybe I spoke up when they thought I should stay quiet, maybe they didn’t like how my husband and I did certain things.\n\nBut provoke them? Like I did something that would justify all this? No. I don’t think so. Nothing that would explain months of this quiet pressure, where it feels like they’re messing with our money and our good name. That’s the part I keep trying to get people to understand. It’s not one big fight where I can point to it and say, “That’s when it started.” It’s these little things that keep happening — billing problems showing up at work that make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting strange toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say it.\n\nSo maybe they have their reasons in their own minds. Maybe they think I took my husband away from them, or they don’t like that I notice things. I don’t know exactly what excuse they use. But I don’t believe I did anything to deserve this. And even when people pull records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t mean nothing is happening. The pattern still points back to them.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or stressful happening in your life before you started noticing these issues with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, there was stress before I really started noticing the pattern. It wasn’t like everything was peaceful and then out of nowhere I decided something was wrong. It started around the trouble with my husband’s family over money and the holidays — expectations about who should pay for what, where we were supposed to be, what we were supposed to do. Those kinds of things.\n\nAnd then after that, over the months, I started seeing how things were connecting. I’d say it’s been going on about nine months now. That’s when it really began to feel like more than ordinary family tension. At first I tried to tell myself, “Okay, families argue, people get upset around holidays, money makes people act funny.” But it didn’t settle down. It kept continuing in these little ways, and that’s when I started feeling like they were involved in what was happening.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation or evidence that would make you question your belief that your husband's family is behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would make me question it, honestly, because people keep acting like if they pull up one record and it looks normal, then that should settle the whole thing. But that doesn’t really answer what I’m talking about.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not one obvious thing where someone signs their name to it. It’s little things, like a billing error showing up at work in a way that makes me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then when I try to explain it, people say, “Well, the records don’t show anything,” or “Maybe it’s just a mistake.” But I know how these things feel when they keep happening and they all point in the same direction.\n\nSo, evidence that would make me question it… I guess if there was some clear explanation for all of it that didn’t involve them, maybe. But so far nobody has given me one that makes sense. They just tell me there’s nothing there, and that feels like missing the point. Because no matter what records anyone pulls up, I still know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts like this before, where you felt like someone was out to get you or sabotaging you?\n\nPatient: Not like this, no. I mean, I’ve had times in life where I felt someone didn’t like me, or where there was tension with people, sure. Everybody has that. But this is different.\n\nThis has been months now of me trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly interfering with our money and our good name. It’s not like I woke up one day and said, “Everyone is against me.” It’s more like one thing happened, then another thing, then another, and after a while you can’t keep calling everything a coincidence. Like little billing errors showing up at work in ways that make me look careless, or people at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something they’re not telling me.\n\nBefore all this, I don’t remember having thoughts like that, not in this same way. I wasn’t walking around thinking people were out to get me. But now, with this situation, no matter what records anyone pulls up or how many people say they don’t see it, I know it ties back to them. That’s the part that makes it so upsetting, because it feels like I’m the only one putting the pieces together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your belief about your husband's family being behind these actions, no matter what evidence or explanations you're given?\n\nPatient: Honestly… I don’t think so. I know that probably sounds stubborn, but it’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It’s just that I’ve lived with this for months now, and I’ve watched the same kinds of things keep happening in different places.\n\nPeople keep saying, “Well, the records look fine,” or, “That could just be a billing mistake,” or, “Maybe that person at church was just having a bad day.” But when little billing errors show up at work in a way that makes me look careless, and then someone at church suddenly acts strange toward me like they’ve heard something, and then another thing happens with money or with my name… after a while it all connects.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether some explanation could change my mind, I guess I’m saying I don’t see how. It’s not going to be sitting there in plain writing with their names on it. That’s why it’s so hard to prove. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have any enemies or people who might want to harm you outside of your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Outside of his family? No, not really. I mean, I can’t say I have enemies in the way people talk about enemies, like someone openly threatening me or saying they want to hurt me. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is his family. That’s where it ties together. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not dramatic, it’s not somebody standing on my porch yelling at me. It’s little things that make me look bad or make life harder, like billing errors showing up at work in a way that makes me seem careless, or people at church suddenly acting different toward me, like they’ve heard something but won’t say what.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s some other person out there I think is after me, I don’t think so. It’s more that other people may get pulled into it, or they may believe things they’ve been told, but the source of it, the people behind it, I really believe that’s my husband’s family. And even when people pull up records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. It still comes back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done something to provoke your husband's family into behaving this way towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, honestly. I’m not sitting here saying I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. Families have disagreements, and with in-laws there are always little tensions, you know? Maybe I didn’t handle every conversation exactly right, maybe I spoke up when they thought I should stay quiet, maybe they didn’t like how my husband and I did certain things.\n\nBut provoke them? Like I did something that would justify all this? No. I don’t think so. Nothing that would explain months of this quiet pressure, where it feels like they’re messing with our money and our good name. That’s the part I keep trying to get people to understand. It’s not one big fight where I can point to it and say, “That’s when it started.” It’s these little things that keep happening — billing problems showing up at work that make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting strange toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say it.\n\nSo maybe they have their reasons in their own minds. Maybe they think I took my husband away from them, or they don’t like that I notice things. I don’t know exactly what excuse they use. But I don’t believe I did anything to deserve this. And even when people pull records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t mean nothing is happening. The pattern still points back to them.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or stressful happening in your life before you started noticing these issues with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, there was stress before I really started noticing the pattern. It wasn’t like everything was peaceful and then out of nowhere I decided something was wrong. It started around the trouble with my husband’s family over money and the holidays — expectations about who should pay for what, where we were supposed to be, what we were supposed to do. Those kinds of things.\n\nAnd then after that, over the months, I started seeing how things were connecting. I’d say it’s been going on about nine months now. That’s when it really began to feel like more than ordinary family tension. At first I tried to tell myself, “Okay, families argue, people get upset around holidays, money makes people act funny.” But it didn’t settle down. It kept continuing in these little ways, and that’s when I started feeling like they were involved in what was happening.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation or evidence that would make you question your belief that your husband's family is behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would make me question it, honestly, because people keep acting like if they pull up one record and it looks normal, then that should settle the whole thing. But that doesn’t really answer what I’m talking about.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not one obvious thing where someone signs their name to it. It’s little things, like a billing error showing up at work in a way that makes me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then when I try to explain it, people say, “Well, the records don’t show anything,” or “Maybe it’s just a mistake.” But I know how these things feel when they keep happening and they all point in the same direction.\n\nSo, evidence that would make me question it… I guess if there was some clear explanation for all of it that didn’t involve them, maybe. But so far nobody has given me one that makes sense. They just tell me there’s nothing there, and that feels like missing the point. Because no matter what records anyone pulls up, I still know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts like this before, where you felt like someone was out to get you or sabotaging you?\n\nPatient: Not like this, no. I mean, I’ve had times in life where I felt someone didn’t like me, or where there was tension with people, sure. Everybody has that. But this is different.\n\nThis has been months now of me trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly interfering with our money and our good name. It’s not like I woke up one day and said, “Everyone is against me.” It’s more like one thing happened, then another thing, then another, and after a while you can’t keep calling everything a coincidence. Like little billing errors showing up at work in ways that make me look careless, or people at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something they’re not telling me.\n\nBefore all this, I don’t remember having thoughts like that, not in this same way. I wasn’t walking around thinking people were out to get me. But now, with this situation, no matter what records anyone pulls up or how many people say they don’t see it, I know it ties back to them. That’s the part that makes it so upsetting, because it feels like I’m the only one putting the pieces together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your belief about your husband's family being behind these actions, no matter what evidence or explanations you're given?\n\nPatient: Honestly… I don’t think so. I know that probably sounds stubborn, but it’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It’s just that I’ve lived with this for months now, and I’ve watched the same kinds of things keep happening in different places.\n\nPeople keep saying, “Well, the records look fine,” or, “That could just be a billing mistake,” or, “Maybe that person at church was just having a bad day.” But when little billing errors show up at work in a way that makes me look careless, and then someone at church suddenly acts strange toward me like they’ve heard something, and then another thing happens with money or with my name… after a while it all connects.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether some explanation could change my mind, I guess I’m saying I don’t see how. Because no matter what records someone pulls up, I still know it ties back to my husband’s family. They’re careful about it. That’s the whole point. It’s not going to be sitting there in plain writing with their names on it. But I know they’re behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your perceptions of your husband's family's actions might be a product of your own mind, rather than based on actual events?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve thought about what people are trying to say when they ask me that, yes. Like, “Could this be in your head?” But that doesn’t feel right to me, because these are actual things that have happened. It’s not like I’m just sitting at home imagining problems out of nowhere.\n\nFor months now I’ve been trying to explain that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. And it’s always done in these small ways that are easy for other people to brush off. Like a billing error shows up at work and suddenly I look careless, or someone at church starts acting strange toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say what. And then if I bring it up, people want to say, “Well, maybe that was just a mistake,” or, “Maybe you misunderstood.” But when it keeps happening, over and over, it’s hard to believe it’s just my own mind making connections.\n\nSo no, I don’t really think it’s just coming from me. I know people may not see it the way I do, and I know the records may not show their names written on anything, but that doesn’t mean they aren’t behind it. They’re careful. Completely wrong? I… no, I don’t think so. I know that sounds like I’m not leaving any room for anything else, but that’s because I’ve been living it, not just guessing.

If it were one thing, maybe. If it were just one billing error at work, I could say, fine, maybe somebody typed something wrong. Or if one person at church acted funny one Sunday, maybe they were tired or had something going on. But it’s been months of these little things, and they all point the same direction. It’s the money, and it’s my name, my reputation, the way people look at me or treat me like they know something I don’t.

And every time somebody says, “Well, the records don’t show that,” I keep thinking, of course they don’t. They’re not going to leave it in writing. My husband’s family is careful. That’s what makes it so hard, because people want proof like a signed confession or something, and real life isn’t always like that.

So, no… I don’t really see a situation where I’d be completely wrong about it. Maybe I could be off about one detail here or there, like exactly who said what or which person did which part, but the bigger thing? That it ties back to them? I know it does.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like I’ve been trying for months to get somebody to really listen to me, and everyone keeps acting like I’m making too much of it. It has to do with my husband’s family. They’ve been quietly interfering with our money and with my reputation, our good name, you know?\n\nIt’s not one big obvious thing where I can just point to it and say, “There, that’s it.” It’s little things that keep happening. Like at work, I’m a billing clerk, so I know when numbers don’t look right, and these little billing errors keep showing up in ways that make me look careless. And then someone will pull up records and say, “Oh, see, it’s nothing,” or “That was just a mistake,” but I know it ties back to them somehow.\n\nAnd then at church, people who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly act strange, like short with me or like they’ve heard something. Nobody comes right out and says it, of course, but that’s kind of the point. It’s all done quietly. I just need someone to understand that this isn’t me being dramatic. It’s been going on for months, and I’m tired of being treated like I’m the problem when I’m the one noticing what’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your husband's family is intentionally trying to harm you or your reputation?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I don’t think it’s accidental anymore, not after how long it’s been going on and how the same kinds of things keep happening in different places. It’s not like they came up to me and said, “We’re going to ruin your name,” obviously they wouldn’t do that. It’s quieter than that.\n\nIt’s things with our money, and then things that make me look bad. Like at work, with the billing errors—small ones, just enough that if someone looks quickly, I seem careless or incompetent. And then people act like, “Well, the records don’t prove anything,” but I work with billing. I know when things don’t sit right. And then at church, people who used to be warm with me suddenly get distant or look at me a certain way, like they’ve heard something about me.\n\nSo yes, I believe his family is behind it. Maybe not in some loud obvious way, but intentionally, yes. It feels like they’re trying to damage how people see me and interfere with what we have.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're the only one being targeted by your husband's family, or could others be affected as well?\n\nPatient: I think I’m the one they’re focusing on, mostly, because it’s my name that keeps getting touched, if that makes sense. It’s our money too, yes, but the way it shows up is always like it’s meant to make me look irresponsible or careless. Like those little billing things at work—small enough that someone else can say, “Oh, that’s just an error,” but placed in a way where I’m the one who looks bad.\n\nAnd at church, it’s me people act funny toward. They don’t say anything outright, which is part of why it’s so hard to explain, but I can feel the shift. People who used to be friendly suddenly get stiff or short, like they’ve heard something about me or been told to keep their distance.\n\nI suppose other people could be affected, because if they’re messing with our finances, that touches my husband too. But the reputation part—the good name part—it feels aimed at me. I’m the one everyone keeps trying to convince is “overreacting,” even though these things have been happening for months and they connect back to his family.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that your husband's family is behind these actions?\n\nPatient: Proof in the way everyone wants, like a signed note or someone admitting it? No, I don’t have that. That’s part of what makes this so frustrating, because every time I try to explain it, people act like if there isn’t one single piece of paper that says, “Yes, we did this,” then it must not be happening.\n\nBut I do have what I’m seeing over and over. For months now, these little things keep happening with our money and with my name. At work, the billing errors don’t feel random to me. I’m a billing clerk, I know how records are supposed to look, and these mistakes show up in ways that make me look careless. Then when someone pulls up the records, they say it’s just a small error or nothing can be proven, but that doesn’t mean there’s no pattern.\n\nAnd at church, people who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly act strange—short, distant, like they’ve heard something. Nobody says it outright, of course. That’s the whole point. It’s quiet. It’s the kind of thing where if you describe one piece by itself, people can dismiss it. But when it keeps happening in different places, and it all seems to come back around to my husband’s family and our good name, I know what it is.\n\nSo no, I don’t have the kind of proof other people keep demanding. But I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these actions by your husband's family could be happening without you noticing or realizing it?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that’s exactly how they do it—quietly, where I don’t always see it happening right in front of me. That’s what I’ve been trying to explain. It’s not like they come to my house and announce it, or do something obvious where I can point and say, “There, that’s them.” It’s more like things happen around me, and then I’m left dealing with the result.\n\nLike with the money, or the little errors that show up at work. I don’t necessarily see someone changing something in the moment, but then there it is, and somehow it reflects badly on me. And at church, same thing—someone suddenly acts funny toward me, or distant, and I don’t know exactly what was said to them, but I can tell something has shifted. People want me to ignore that, but when it keeps happening for months, in different places, it isn’t just nothing.\n\nSo yes, I think some of it could be happening without me realizing it right away. That’s part of what makes it so upsetting. I notice after—the look someone gives me, the way a record doesn’t sit right, the way my good name gets chipped away at little by little. And no matter what records people pull up, I know it ties back to my husband’s family.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your husband's family might not be behind these things you've been noticing?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose if you’re asking me in a general way, could there be some other explanation for one little thing here or there, then maybe. I’m not saying every single odd look from every person in the world is them personally doing something. But when you put it all together, after months of it, I don’t really see how I’m supposed to believe it’s just random.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, honestly. I’ve been trying to get people to understand that it’s not one isolated billing error or one person at church being a little cool toward me. It’s the way these things keep happening and they all touch the same areas—our money, my reputation, our good name. At work, small billing things come up in a way that makes me look careless, and then if records get pulled, people say, “Well, this doesn’t prove anything.” And at church, someone who used to be perfectly normal with me suddenly acts stiff or funny, like they’ve heard something they’re not saying out loud.\n\nSo could someone tell me, “Maybe it isn’t your husband’s family”? Yes, people tell me that all the time. But no matter what records anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. It’s too connected, and it’s been going on too long for me to just shrug it off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could convince you that your husband's family is not behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me, honestly, because every time someone says they’ve checked something, it’s like they’re only checking the obvious part. They pull up a record or look at one billing issue and say, “See, there’s no proof,” but that doesn’t explain why these things keep happening around my money and my name.\n\nI’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is doing this quietly. Not in a way where they leave fingerprints all over it, but in the way little errors show up at work, or someone at church suddenly starts acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then I’m the one who looks careless, or unstable, or like I’m imagining it.\n\nSo if you’re asking, could there be some record that would make me say, “Oh, it definitely wasn’t them”? I don’t really see how, because the records are exactly the kind of thing that can miss what’s really going on. No matter what anyone pulls up, I know it ties back to them. It’s the pattern, and it’s been going on too long.\n\nDoctor: Could anything change your mind about your husband's family being behind these actions?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think about that, I really have, because people keep asking me like there must be some simple answer that would make me drop it. But the problem is, when someone says, “Well, we checked the records,” or “That billing issue was just a mistake,” it doesn’t cover the whole thing. It’s not just one record or one mistake.\n\nI’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is doing this quietly, in ways that don’t look obvious if you only look at each thing by itself. Like the little billing errors that show up at work and somehow make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they know something or heard something about me. And then if I say anything, I’m the one who sounds like I’m overreacting.\n\nSo could something change my mind? I don’t know. Maybe if all of it stopped completely and people went back to treating me normal, and there weren’t these little things with money or my good name being picked at. But as far as someone just pulling up records and saying, “See, it isn’t them,” no. That wouldn’t change it for me, because I know it ties back to them. It’s the pattern of it. That’s what people keep missing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done anything to unintentionally contribute to your husband's family's behavior towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, because I don’t want to pretend I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. I suppose maybe I’ve been too direct at times, or maybe I noticed things and started asking questions, and they didn’t like that. But I don’t think anything I did justifies what’s been going on.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not like they come out and say it plainly. It’s little things, like billing errors showing up at work that make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something they’re not saying. And then when I try to explain it, people act like I’m making too much of it.\n\nSo if you mean, did I unintentionally cause it somehow… maybe by not just keeping quiet, maybe by standing up for myself, I don’t know. But no, I don’t think I caused them to do this. They chose to do it. And no matter what records anyone pulls up or how they try to explain away each little piece, I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting some of these events or signs?\n\nPatient: I understand why you’re asking that, and I’ve tried to be fair about it. I know people can misread things sometimes. If it was just one thing, like one billing error, maybe I could say, alright, maybe I’m taking it the wrong way. Or if one person at church seemed off one Sunday, maybe they were just having a bad day.\n\nBut that’s not what this feels like. It’s been months of these little things, and they all circle back to the same place. I’m here because I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not loud or obvious, it’s not like they come out and say, “We’re doing this.” It’s the way little billing problems show up at work and make me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acts funny toward me, like they’ve heard something about me but won’t say it.\n\nSo, could I be misinterpreting one small thing here or there? Maybe, in theory. I’m not saying I never misunderstand anything. But the whole pattern? No, I don’t think I’m wrong about that. And when people just pull up records and say, “See, there’s nothing there,” that doesn’t really answer it for me, because those records don’t show the quiet parts. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to consider an alternative explanation for the events you've described, other than your husband's family being behind them?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can try to consider it, because I know that’s what you’re asking me to do, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I’ve gone over it in my head so many times, actually. Like, could the billing errors at work just be ordinary mistakes? Could someone at church just be in a bad mood or distracted? Could I be connecting things that other people don’t see connected? I understand those are possibilities if you look at each piece by itself.\n\nBut that’s exactly the problem. Everyone keeps looking at each thing separately, like one little accounting problem here, one strange look there, one conversation that changes when I walk up. And I’m telling you, when you’re living inside it, it doesn’t feel separate. It feels coordinated. It feels like it keeps coming back to my husband’s family, even if they’re careful enough that it doesn’t show plainly on paper.\n\nSo yes, I can say the words that there could be another explanation for one or two things. But for the whole thing? No, not really. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. And no matter what records anyone pulls up, that doesn’t erase the pattern. I know it ties back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have any enemies or people who might want to harm you outside of your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Outside of his family? No, not really. I mean, I can’t say I have enemies in the way people talk about enemies, like someone openly threatening me or saying they want to hurt me. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I keep coming back to is his family. That’s where it ties together. I’m here because for months I’ve been trying to get people to understand that they’re quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s not dramatic, it’s not somebody standing on my porch yelling at me. It’s little things that make me look bad or make life harder, like billing errors showing up at work in a way that makes me seem careless, or people at church suddenly acting different toward me, like they’ve heard something but won’t say what.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there’s some other person out there I think is after me, I don’t think so. It’s more that other people may get pulled into it, or they may believe things they’ve been told, but the source of it, the people behind it, I really believe that’s my husband’s family. And even when people pull up records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t change what I’m seeing. It still comes back to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've done something to provoke your husband's family into behaving this way towards you?\n\nPatient: I’ve asked myself that, honestly. I’m not sitting here saying I’m perfect or that I’ve never rubbed anyone the wrong way. Families have disagreements, and with in-laws there are always little tensions, you know? Maybe I didn’t handle every conversation exactly right, maybe I spoke up when they thought I should stay quiet, maybe they didn’t like how my husband and I did certain things.\n\nBut provoke them? Like I did something that would justify all this? No. I don’t think so. Nothing that would explain months of this quiet pressure, where it feels like they’re messing with our money and our good name. And even when people pull records and say there’s nothing there, that doesn’t mean nothing is happening. The pattern still points back to them.\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or stressful happening in your life before you started noticing these issues with your husband's family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, there was stress before I really started noticing the pattern. It started around the trouble with my husband’s family over money and the holidays — expectations about who should pay for what, where we were supposed to be, what we were supposed to do. But that doesn’t really answer what I’m talking about.\n\nI mean, I’m here because for months now I’ve been trying to get people to understand that my husband’s family is quietly messing with our money and our good name. It’s little things, like a billing error showing up at work in a way that makes me look careless, or someone at church suddenly acting funny toward me, like they’ve heard something. And then when I try to explain it, people say, “Well, the records don’t show anything,” or “Maybe it’s just a mistake.” But I know how these things feel when they keep happening and they all point in the same direction.\n\nSo, evidence that would make me question it… I guess if there was some clear explanation for all of it that didn’t involve them, maybe.
